It feels right there with me, and it’s been really upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Around the time the voices started, did you also have a period where you felt unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. It’s like I’d get this feeling like I was bright, like everything was opening up and Greenville couldn’t even hold all the plans I had in my head. Like I was chosen for something, you know? And then real quick I’d flip—somebody would ask me a little thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself, and I’d just snap hard at them. Then later I’d be thinking, why did I get so mad over that?\n\nDoctor: Had you been using any drugs around the time the voices started?\n\nPatient: Drugs? I mean… I don’t really know how to answer that. I wasn’t thinking about it like that at the time. People say “drugs” and I think of folks hanging around the corner near the bus stop, and I’m not like that—I’m usually just trying to get to work and keep my apartment together. But around when it started, I’m not sure what all counts or what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you used meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or taken pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. Before the voices started, I was just trying to get through my shifts and get home, and people offer things sometimes, or talk about things, but I don’t want to say yes to something if I’m not sure. \n\nPills too, I mean, everybody has pills in their cabinet, my aunt used to keep all hers in a little butter cookie tin, you know those blue tins? But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.

inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There have been times on client visits where I’d stop what I was supposed to be doing and start lining up gloves, pill cups, and crochet scraps in patterns, or stand in the hallway staring like I was waiting for some signal.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: When I’m not all wound up, I can sit for hours with my coffee getting cold, not answering calls, not going to the walking club, and not even caring to pick up my paintbrush.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. that the other home-care agency and some people from the library were watching my paintings and client papers to steal a chance that was meant for me, and even when folks told me it made no sense I perception.\n  manifestation: I came in because I keep hearing voices clear as somebody standing in my little studio, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged, even when the house is quiet and nobody is there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There have been times on client visits where I’d stop what I was supposed to be doing and start lining up gloves, pill cups, and crochet scraps in patterns, or stand in the hallway staring like I was waiting for some signal.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: When I’m not all wound up, I can sit for hours with my coffee getting cold, not answering calls, not going to the walking club, and not even caring to pick up my paintbrush.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing these voices, like clear as if somebody’s standing right there in my little studio. It’ll be quiet, nobody there, and I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged. And I know how that sounds, but it’s not like the TV left on or somebody outside—my neighbor does bang around sometimes, she’s always moving furniture or something—but this is different. It feels right there with me, and it’s been really upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Around the time the voices started, did you also have a period where you felt unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. It’s like I’d get this feeling like I was bright, like everything was opening up and Greenville couldn’t even hold all the plans I had in my head. Like I was chosen for something, you know? And then real quick I’d flip—somebody would ask me a little thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself, and I’d just snap hard at them. Then later I’d be thinking, why did I get so mad over that?\n\nDoctor: Had you been using any drugs around the time the voices started?\n\nPatient: Drugs? I mean… I don’t really know how to answer that. I wasn’t thinking about it like that at the time. People say “drugs” and I think of folks hanging around the corner near the bus stop, and I’m not like that—I’m usually just trying to get to work and keep my apartment together. But around when it started, I’m not sure what all counts or what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you used meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or taken pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.

Sometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?

So… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.

I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. Before the voices started, I was just trying to get through my shifts and get home, and people offer things sometimes, or talk about things, but I don’t want to say yes to something if I’m not sure. \n\nPills too, I mean, everybody has pills in their cabinet, my aunt used to keep all hers in a little butter cookie tin, you know those blue tins? But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.

I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. Nobody told me it was serious. And seizures… I don’t think so. I’m not sure what all counts as a seizure, though.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you lost interest in almost everything you usually enjoy for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.\n\nTwo weeks is the part I get stuck on. Because with my work, the days kind of blend together, and I’ll be at one house helping somebody with their bath or their lunch, and then I come home and it’s already late, and I think, well, maybe I didn’t enjoy anything, but maybe I was just tired.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure that I lost interest in almost everything for two whole weeks. I don’t know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I don’t know.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring the times you felt unusually energized and needed very little sleep, were you using meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t thinking of it like that at the time, like checking off what I had or hadn’t used.

Pills, I’m careful about because people offer you things sometimes, you know, like at work somebody says, “Oh this helped my back,” and I have back pain from lifting folks, but I don’t like taking random stuff. And cannabis, meth, cocaine… I don’t know, I don’t want to say the wrong thing.

Those times I was up, I just remember being up and feeling like I had to keep moving. I can’t really tie it to something like that for sure.

And I know how that sounds, but it’s not like the TV left on or somebody outside—my neighbor does bang around sometimes, she’s always moving furniture or something—but this is different. It feels right there with me, and it’s been really upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Around the time the voices started, did you also have a period where you felt unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. It’s like I’d get this feeling like I was bright, like everything was opening up and Greenville couldn’t even hold all the plans I had in my head. Like I was chosen for something, you know? I mean… I don’t really know how to answer that. I wasn’t thinking about it like that at the time. People say “drugs” and I think of folks hanging around the corner near the bus stop, and I’m not like that—I’m usually just trying to get to work and keep my apartment together. But around when it started, I’m not sure what all counts or what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you used meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or taken pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. Before the voices started, I was just trying to get through my shifts and get home, and people offer things sometimes, or talk about things, but I don’t want to say yes to something if I’m not sure. \n\nPills too, I mean, everybody has pills in their cabinet, my aunt used to keep all hers in a little butter cookie tin, you know those blue tins? But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. I also stopped going to my walking club and library art talks, so I was mostly alone at home.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, since my work hours were cut and my money situation got much worse.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 9 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed fears about my work being watched have been happening for about 9 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being charged up, sleeping very little, and chasing big plans have been coming and going for about 11 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. Nobody told me it was serious. And seizures… I don’t think so. I’m not sure what all counts as a seizure, though.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you lost interest in almost everything you usually enjoy for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.\n\nTwo weeks is the part I get stuck on. Because with my work, the days kind of blend together, and I’ll be at one house helping somebody with their bath or their lunch, and then I come home and it’s already late, and I think, well, maybe I didn’t enjoy anything, but maybe I was just tired.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure that I lost interest in almost everything for two whole weeks. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: During the times you felt unusually energized and needed very little sleep, were you using meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t thinking of it like that at the time, like checking off what I had or hadn’t used.\n\nPills, I’m careful about because people offer you things sometimes, you know, like at work somebody says, “Oh this helped my back,” and I have back pain from lifting folks, but I don’t like taking random stuff. And cannabis, meth, cocaine… I don’t know, I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nThose times I was up, I just remember being up and feeling like I had to keep moving. I can’t really tie it to something like that for sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring the past nine months, was there ever a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I don’t know… depressed is a big word, you know? Like, I get down, sure, but nearly every day for two weeks, I’m not sure how I’d even count that.

But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. Nobody told me it was serious. And seizures… I don’t think so. I’m not sure what all counts as a seizure, though.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you lost interest in almost everything you usually enjoy for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.\n\nTwo weeks is the part I get stuck on. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: During the times you felt unusually energized and needed very little sleep, were you using meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing these voices, like clear as if somebody’s standing right there in my little studio. It’ll be quiet, nobody there, and I hear them saying my name and saying my work is being judged. And I know how that sounds, but it’s not like the TV left on or somebody outside—my neighbor does bang around sometimes, she’s always moving furniture or something—but this is different. It feels right there with me, and it’s been really upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Around the time the voices started, did you also have a period where you felt unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. It’s like I’d get this feeling like I was bright, like everything was opening up and Greenville couldn’t even hold all the plans I had in my head. Like I was chosen for something, you know? I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. Nobody told me it was serious. And seizures… I don’t think so. I’m not sure what all counts as a seizure, though.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you lost interest in almost everything you usually enjoy for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.\n\nTwo weeks is the part I get stuck on. Because with my work, the days kind of blend together, and I’ll be at one house helping somebody with their bath or their lunch, and then I come home and it’s already late, and I think, well, maybe I didn’t enjoy anything, but maybe I was just tired.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure that I lost interest in almost everything for two whole weeks. Cannabis… I mean, people talk about it like it’s nothing now, like everybody’s got gummies or somebody’s nephew grows it, and I hear that a lot at work, especially when folks are hurting or can’t sleep.

For me, I don’t really keep track of it like that. I’m not saying yes exactly, I just… I don’t know what you’re counting. Like smoke? edibles? being around it? I’ve been around people using it, sure, but I can’t give you a clean answer.

It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I don’t keep track good. And I know how that sounds, but it’s not like the TV left on or somebody outside—my neighbor does bang around sometimes, she’s always moving furniture or something—but this is different. It feels right there with me, and it’s been really upsetting.\n\nDoctor: Around the time the voices started, did you also have a period where you felt unusually energized or high?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I did. But around when it started, I’m not sure what all counts or what you mean exactly.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, had you used meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or taken pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I mean, I know the names you’re saying, but I can’t really line it up in my head like that. But for me… I can’t really give you a clear answer on those specific things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing the voices?\n\nPatient: About… about nine months, I think. It started and then it just didn’t really leave. The voices, and that feeling like my work is being watched—like somebody’s keeping track of me there. \n\nNine months is what I keep coming back to, though. I remember because I was still trying to keep my schedule straight, and time gets funny when you’re tired, but… yeah, around nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Did the voices begin while you were using any street drugs or pills not prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, doctor. I keep trying to put it in order, like “this happened, then that happened,” but it’s all kind of mixed up in my head. I know you’re asking if it was like street drugs or somebody else’s pills right when the voices started, but I can’t give you a clean yes or no.\n\nBack then I was mostly thinking about work and getting through the day. People talk about stuff, people offer things sometimes, and then you got family with medicine bottles everywhere—I remember my aunt had so many little bottles lined up, and she’d get mad if anybody touched them—but for me, I don’t know that I can say the voices began while I was using anything like that. I just don’t have it clear enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things like seeing people, work, or basic daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. When I’m not all wound up, I can just kind of sit there for hours, with my coffee sitting right next to me getting cold. I’ll hear the phone and just… not answer it. \n\nI used to go to the walking club sometimes, you know, those ladies with the matching sneakers, they’d always talk about whose knees were bad—but I haven’t been going. And my paintbrushes are right there, I can see them, and I just don’t even care to pick one up. It’s like everything feels too far away.\n\nDoctor: Have the voices ever continued during a time when your mood felt normal—not especially high, energized, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think that did happen. There was a stretch after I wasn’t so… revved up, I guess. My energy had dropped back down, and I was sleeping more like a regular person again—not all over the place.\n\nBut the voices didn’t stop then. They kept on for weeks, and I still had that feeling at work like people were judging what I was doing, or keeping track of me somehow. Like even when my mood felt more normal, that part was still there.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because when you’re working in people’s homes, everybody does watch how you do things a little—like if you fold towels wrong or put the cups in the wrong cabinet, they notice—but this felt like more than that. It kept going even when I didn’t feel especially up or down.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least two weeks when you were hearing the voices even though your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, there was. After that time when my energy kind of came down and I wasn’t staying up so much—like I was sleeping more normal, making breakfast at a normal hour, not rearranging things in the middle of the night—the voices were still there.\n\nIt wasn’t just a day or two. It went on for several weeks, I think. And I still had that feeling at work, like people were judging what I was doing or keeping track of me somehow. You know, in home care people do notice little things—where you put the towels, if you wiped the counter right—but this felt like more than that. Even when my mood wasn’t especially high or low, it kept going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed like that, most of the day every day for two weeks, I’m not sure I can say that. I have days where I feel real low and heavy, like even getting dressed feels like too much, but then I’ll get distracted by something, or I’ll have to go do for somebody else, and it’s not the same all day.\n\nTwo weeks is hard for me to count. I don’t keep track good. I’ll think it was a whole month and then my sister says, “No, that was last Tuesday,” so… I don’t want to tell you wrong.\n\nDoctor: During these past nine months, have you ever had a time when you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about that. I know I’ve felt sad, yeah, and empty sometimes, but “most of the day nearly every day” is hard for me to say.\n\nSometimes I think it’s been going on forever, but then the days all run together, especially when I’m working and going from one person’s house to another. Like one lady I help, she keeps all her mail stacked on the kitchen chair, and I’ll be thinking about that instead of how I feel, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. I’ve had low days, but I can’t tell you it was two straight weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there ever a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There were nights I only slept maybe two or three hours, and then I’d get up like I’d had a whole night’s rest, just charged up.\n\nI’d go right back to painting, or I’d start making calls, like it was a normal time to be doing all that. And I remember thinking, well, if I’m awake I might as well get things done… but it was a lot of nights like that, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Before the voices started, did you have any seizure, head injury, or other serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not that I remember. I didn’t have, like, some big accident or anything that sticks out to me before all that.\n\nI’ve bumped my head plenty of times, you know, everybody does—cabinet doors, car doors, one time at work I hit it on a shelf reaching for towels—but I don’t know if that counts. Nobody told me it was serious. And seizures… I don’t think so. I’m not sure what all counts as a seizure, though.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you lost interest in almost everything you usually enjoy for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to measure that. I mean, there’s times I don’t feel like doing things, but then I might still sit there with the TV on or mess with my paints a little, or I’ll think about doing it and not actually do it.\n\nTwo weeks is the part I get stuck on. Because with my work, the days kind of blend together, and I’ll be at one house helping somebody with their bath or their lunch, and then I come home and it’s already late, and I think, well, maybe I didn’t enjoy anything, but maybe I was just tired.\n\nSo… I can’t say for sure that I lost interest in almost everything for two whole weeks. I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: During the times you felt unusually energized and needed very little sleep, were you using meth, cocaine, cannabis, LSD, or pills that weren’t prescribed to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I mean, I wasn’t thinking of it like that at the time, like checking off what I had or hadn’t used.\n\nPills, I’m careful about because people offer you things sometimes, you know, like at work somebody says, “Oh this helped my back,” and I have back pain from lifting folks, but I don’t like taking random stuff. And cannabis, meth, cocaine… I don’t know, I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nThose times I was up, I just remember being up and feeling like I had to keep moving. I can’t really tie it to something like that for sure.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, was there ever a time when you felt depressed nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… depressed is a big word, you know?
